# S5.1 P2 Paper 01 — Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction

## Metadata

- **Title:** Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction
- **Year:** 2023
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2305.08144
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No
- **Corrected action:** Keep as preprint
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** mobile GUI benchmark
- **S5.1 role:** GUI/mobile-agent benchmark
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `mobileenvbuildingqualified2023`

---

## Simple understanding

Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction evaluates or surveys mobile/Android/smartphone agents. Its main value is testing whether agents can perceive phone screens, understand user goals, and execute multi-step UI actions.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark mobile GUI agents under realistic task, screen, and action conditions.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
mobile GUI benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Mobile agents require strong visual grounding, action selection, state tracking, and recovery; current systems often remain brittle on realistic mobile workflows.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,
- stateful workplace or enterprise workflows,
- safety and harmful action detection,
- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Task categories and app/domain coverage.**
- **Observation and action space definition.**
- **Task success or step-level evaluation metrics.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Mobile UI tasks may not generalize directly to desktop web automation.
- **Limitation 2:** Benchmarks can become stale as apps update their interfaces.
- **Limitation 3:** Success depends heavily on observation format, action space, and evaluation rules.

General limitation for your thesis:

```text
A benchmark evaluates only a slice of agent behavior.
No single benchmark proves that an agent can perform generalized web automation and data extraction.
```

Therefore, use this paper as a **P2 supporting source**, not as the sole basis for a central claim.

---

## Venue/status caution

This should be cited as a preprint or technical report unless a later peer-reviewed version is confirmed.

For thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed papers → can support stronger claims
workshop/demo papers → useful but cite as workshop/system evidence
arXiv/technical reports → useful for recent trends, but cite cautiously
unclear/submission papers → verify again before final submission
```

---

## Relation to S5.1

This paper belongs in **S5.1** because S5.1 is about how LLM-based agents are evaluated.

Its role is:

```text
Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction
→ mobile GUI benchmark
→ P2 support for agent benchmark/evaluation discussion
```

Use this paper after explaining its benchmark/evaluation design.  
Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce what it measures first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because:

It helps compare web agents with broader GUI/mobile agents, especially for perception, grounding, and action evaluation.

For your thesis, the paper can support evaluation discussion around:

- what tasks agents should be tested on,
- whether evaluation should be static or interactive,
- whether success should be judged by final answer, trajectory, state, or evidence,
- how to measure failures in planning, grounding, memory, and tool use,
- and why real-world agent evaluation remains unsolved.

---

## How to use this paper in the literature review

Recommended use:

- Use it as **P2 support** in S5.1.
- Extract one exact benchmark property or metric.
- Extract one limitation or failure mode.
- Compare it with P0/P1 benchmarks, not as a replacement.
- If the paper is preprint/workshop, phrase claims cautiously.

Suggested thesis sentence:

> Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction contributes to the evaluation literature by focusing on **mobile GUI benchmark**, showing that agent assessment must consider not only final task success but also environment realism, interaction trajectories, tool/action correctness, and reliability.

---

# S5.1 P2 Paper 03 — TravelPlanner: A Benchmark for Real-World Planning with Language Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** TravelPlanner: A Benchmark for Real-World Planning with Language Agents
- **Year:** 2024
- **Verified venue/status:** ICML 2024 Spotlight
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Corrected action:** Upgrade to peer-reviewed
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** real-world planning benchmark
- **S5.1 role:** agent evaluation methodology source
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `travelplannerrealworldplanning2024`

---

## Simple understanding

TravelPlanner evaluates whether language agents can produce realistic travel plans under constraints such as time, location, budget, and user preferences. It is important because it exposes the weakness of LLM agents on long-horizon planning in a real-world-like domain.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Formulate travel planning as a realistic benchmark where agents must satisfy multiple constraints and generate coherent itineraries.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
real-world planning benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

LLM agents can produce plausible plans, but they often violate constraints, miss dependencies, or fail to maintain consistency across long-horizon planning steps.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,

- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Task formulation with real-world travel constraints.**
- **Evaluation protocol for constraint satisfaction and plan quality.**
- **Failure cases showing long-horizon inconsistency.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Travel planning is one domain; results may not generalize to all web or enterprise tasks.
- **Limitation 2:** Plans can look fluent while still violating hidden constraints.
- **Limitation 3:** The benchmark evaluates planning output more than real browser execution.

General limitation for your thesis:

# S5.1 P2 Paper 05 — WorkBench: a Benchmark Dataset for Agents in a Realistic Workplace Setting

## Metadata

- **Title:** WorkBench: a Benchmark Dataset for Agents in a Realistic Workplace Setting
- **Year:** 2024
- **Verified venue/status:** COLM 2024
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Corrected action:** Upgrade to peer-reviewed
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** workplace agent benchmark
- **S5.1 role:** computer-use / enterprise / productivity benchmark
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `workbenchdatasetagentsrealistic2024`

---

## Simple understanding

WorkBench: a Benchmark Dataset for Agents in a Realistic Workplace Setting evaluates computer-use, desktop, office, or workplace agents. Its main value is testing whether agents can complete realistic software tasks in stateful digital workspaces.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark agents on desktop or workplace workflows involving UI interaction, application state, documents, files, or enterprise tools.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
workplace agent benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Computer-use agents need more than reasoning: they need UI grounding, state management, app-specific knowledge, and safe execution.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,
- stateful workplace or enterprise workflows,
- safety and harmful action detection,
- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Environment and application coverage.**
- **State-based or rule-based evaluation method.**
- **Task success, cost, latency, or safety metrics.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Results may be environment-specific.
- **Limitation 2:** Office/workplace tasks often require exact state changes that are hard for agents to verify.
- **Limitation 3:** Live or high-fidelity environments increase realism but also evaluation complexity.

General limitation for your thesis:

```text
A benchmark evaluates only a slice of agent behavior.
No single benchmark proves that an agent can perform generalized web automation and data extraction.
```

Therefore, use this paper as a **P2 supporting source**, not as the sole basis for a central claim.

---

## Venue/status caution

This is safer for stronger thesis claims because the verified status is **COLM 2024**.

For thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed papers → can support stronger claims
workshop/demo papers → useful but cite as workshop/system evidence
arXiv/technical reports → useful for recent trends, but cite cautiously
unclear/submission papers → verify again before final submission
```

---

```text
→ P2 support for agent benchmark/evaluation discussion
```

---

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because:

It extends evaluation from web tasks to realistic digital work environments, which matters for generalized automation.

For your thesis, the paper can support evaluation discussion around:

- what tasks agents should be tested on,
- whether evaluation should be static or interactive,
- whether success should be judged by final answer, trajectory, state, or evidence,
- how to measure failures in planning, grounding, memory, and tool use,
- and why real-world agent evaluation remains unsolved.

---

- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `aiagentsthatmatter2024`

---

## Simple understanding

AI Agents That Matter is an evaluation-methodology paper arguing that agent evaluations often suffer from weak baselines, inconsistent protocols, and overclaiming.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Provide methodological guidance for evaluating AI agents rigorously and meaningfully.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
agent evaluation methodology
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Agent results can be misleading when evaluations ignore cost, robustness, reproducibility, environment setup, ablations, and baseline quality.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,

- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

Also extract, if available:

- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

- **Limitation 1:** It is mainly methodological rather than a new web-agent benchmark.
- **Limitation 2:** It does not itself solve benchmark standardization.
- **Limitation 3:** Recommendations require extra effort and may be hard to apply to very new agent systems.

General limitation for your thesis:

# S5.1 P2 Paper 09 — CRAB: Cross-environment Agent Benchmark for Multimodal Language Model Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** CRAB: Cross-environment Agent Benchmark for Multimodal Language Model Agents
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** Findings of ACL 2025
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Corrected action:** Upgrade to peer-reviewed Findings
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** cross-environment multimodal agent benchmark
- **S5.1 role:** agent evaluation methodology source
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `crabcrossenvironmentagent2025`

---

## Simple understanding

CRAB: Cross-environment Agent Benchmark for Multimodal Language Model Agents evaluates computer-use, desktop, office, or workplace agents. Its main value is testing whether agents can complete realistic software tasks in stateful digital workspaces.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark agents on desktop or workplace workflows involving UI interaction, application state, documents, files, or enterprise tools.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
cross-environment multimodal agent benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Computer-use agents need more than reasoning: they need UI grounding, state management, app-specific knowledge, and safe execution.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,
- stateful workplace or enterprise workflows,
- safety and harmful action detection,
- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Environment and application coverage.**
- **State-based or rule-based evaluation method.**
- **Task success, cost, latency, or safety metrics.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Results may be environment-specific.
- **Limitation 2:** Office/workplace tasks often require exact state changes that are hard for agents to verify.
- **Limitation 3:** Live or high-fidelity environments increase realism but also evaluation complexity.

General limitation for your thesis:

```text
A benchmark evaluates only a slice of agent behavior.
No single benchmark proves that an agent can perform generalized web automation and data extraction.
```

Therefore, use this paper as a **P2 supporting source**, not as the sole basis for a central claim.

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
```

# S5.1 P2 Paper 13 — SPA-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for SmartPhone Agent Evaluation

## Metadata

- **Title:** SPA-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for SmartPhone Agent Evaluation
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** ICLR 2025 Spotlight
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Corrected action:** Upgrade to peer-reviewed
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** smartphone agent benchmark
- **S5.1 role:** GUI/mobile-agent benchmark
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `spabenchcomprehensivesmartphone2025`

---

## Simple understanding

SPA-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for SmartPhone Agent Evaluation evaluates or surveys mobile/Android/smartphone agents. Its main value is testing whether agents can perceive phone screens, understand user goals, and execute multi-step UI actions.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark mobile GUI agents under realistic task, screen, and action conditions.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
smartphone agent benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Mobile agents require strong visual grounding, action selection, state tracking, and recovery; current systems often remain brittle on realistic mobile workflows.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,
- stateful workplace or enterprise workflows,
- safety and harmful action detection,
- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Task categories and app/domain coverage.**
- **Observation and action space definition.**
- **Task success or step-level evaluation metrics.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Mobile UI tasks may not generalize directly to desktop web automation.
- **Limitation 2:** Benchmarks can become stale as apps update their interfaces.
- **Limitation 3:** Success depends heavily on observation format, action space, and evaluation rules.

General limitation for your thesis:

```text
A benchmark evaluates only a slice of agent behavior.
No single benchmark proves that an agent can perform generalized web automation and data extraction.
```

Therefore, use this paper as a **P2 supporting source**, not as the sole basis for a central claim.

---

## Venue/status caution

This is safer for stronger thesis claims because the verified status is **ICLR 2025 Spotlight**.

For thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed papers → can support stronger claims
workshop/demo papers → useful but cite as workshop/system evidence
arXiv/technical reports → useful for recent trends, but cite cautiously
unclear/submission papers → verify again before final submission
```

---

## Relation to S5.1

This paper belongs in **S5.1** because S5.1 is about how LLM-based agents are evaluated.

Its role is:

```text
SPA-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for SmartPhone Agent Evaluation
→ smartphone agent benchmark
→ P2 support for agent benchmark/evaluation discussion
```

Use this paper after explaining its benchmark/evaluation design.  
Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce what it measures first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because:

It helps compare web agents with broader GUI/mobile agents, especially for perception, grounding, and action evaluation.

For your thesis, the paper can support evaluation discussion around:

- what tasks agents should be tested on,
- whether evaluation should be static or interactive,
- whether success should be judged by final answer, trajectory, state, or evidence,
- how to measure failures in planning, grounding, memory, and tool use,
- and why real-world agent evaluation remains unsolved.

---

## How to use this paper in the literature review

Recommended use:

- Use it as **P2 support** in S5.1.
- Extract one exact benchmark property or metric.
- Extract one limitation or failure mode.

# S5.1 P2 Paper 14 — Agent-as-a-Judge: Evaluate Agents with Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** Agent-as-a-Judge: Evaluate Agents with Agents
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** ICML 2025
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Corrected action:** Upgrade to peer-reviewed
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** agent-as-judge evaluation
- **S5.1 role:** agent evaluation methodology source
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `agentjudgeevaluateagents2025`

---

## Simple understanding

Agent-as-a-Judge studies whether agents themselves can be used to evaluate other agents. It responds to the difficulty of grading long, interactive trajectories.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Use agentic evaluators that inspect trajectories, interact with environments or tools, and judge task completion.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
agent-as-judge evaluation
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Agent-based judging can better match complex task evaluation than simple output-only LLM judges, but reliability and bias remain concerns.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,

- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

- **Agent-judge architecture.**
- **Agreement with human or rule-based evaluation.**
- **Examples where simple LLM judging fails.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Judges can inherit model biases and hallucinations.
- **Limitation 2:** Agent-as-judge can be expensive due to extra tool use and reasoning.
- **Limitation 3:** Evaluation quality depends on judge prompts, evidence access, and task rubrics.

General limitation for your thesis:


# S5.1 P2 Paper 17 — WebOlympus: An Open Platform for Web Agents on Live Websites

## Metadata

- **Title:** WebOlympus: An Open Platform for Web Agents on Live Websites
- **Year:** 2024
- **Verified venue/status:** EMNLP 2024 System Demonstrations
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes, demo
- **Corrected action:** Upgrade to peer-reviewed demo
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** live website web-agent platform
- **S5.1 role:** web-agent and information-access benchmark
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `webolympusopenplatformweb2024`

---

## Simple understanding

WebOlympus: An Open Platform for Web Agents on Live Websites evaluates web agents or web-based information tasks. Its main value is testing whether agents can search, browse, traverse, retrieve, synthesize, or act on web environments.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark LLM-based agents on web tasks requiring navigation, retrieval, reasoning, and final answer or task completion.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
live website web-agent platform
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Web agents often perform well on short/simple tasks but fail on long-horizon navigation, dynamic pages, multilingual content, broad search, or noisy evidence.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,
- stateful workplace or enterprise workflows,
- safety and harmful action detection,
- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Task/environment design.**
- **Evaluation metrics such as success rate, key-point recall, answer accuracy, NDCG/Recall, or trajectory completion.**
- **Failure analysis for browsing or retrieval mistakes.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Web environments are dynamic and hard to reproduce.
- **Limitation 2:** LLM-based or trajectory-based evaluation can be noisy.
- **Limitation 3:** Strong results may depend on specific scaffolds, browsers, websites, or task selection.

General limitation for your thesis:

```text
A benchmark evaluates only a slice of agent behavior.
No single benchmark proves that an agent can perform generalized web automation and data extraction.
```

Therefore, use this paper as a **P2 supporting source**, not as the sole basis for a central claim.

---

## Venue/status caution

This is safer for stronger thesis claims because the verified status is **EMNLP 2024 System Demonstrations**.

For thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed papers → can support stronger claims
workshop/demo papers → useful but cite as workshop/system evidence
arXiv/technical reports → useful for recent trends, but cite cautiously
unclear/submission papers → verify again before final submission
```

---

## Relation to S5.1

This paper belongs in **S5.1** because S5.1 is about how LLM-based agents are evaluated.

Its role is:

```text
WebOlympus: An Open Platform for Web Agents on Live Websites
→ live website web-agent platform
→ P2 support for agent benchmark/evaluation discussion
```

Use this paper after explaining its benchmark/evaluation design.  
Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce what it measures first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because:

It directly supports the evaluation of generalized web automation and web data extraction agents.

For your thesis, the paper can support evaluation discussion around:

- what tasks agents should be tested on,
- whether evaluation should be static or interactive,
- whether success should be judged by final answer, trajectory, state, or evidence,
- how to measure failures in planning, grounding, memory, and tool use,
- and why real-world agent evaluation remains unsolved.

---

## How to use this paper in the literature review

Recommended use:

- Use it as **P2 support** in S5.1.
- Extract one exact benchmark property or metric.
- Extract one limitation or failure mode.
- Compare it with P0/P1 benchmarks, not as a replacement.

---

## Simple understanding

SWE-bench Goes Live! contributes to agent evaluation by introducing, analyzing, or standardizing a benchmark or evaluation methodology.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Evaluate LLM-based agents under a specific task family, environment, or scoring protocol.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
live software-engineering benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Agent performance depends strongly on environment realism, task horizon, scaffold design, evaluation metric, and failure analysis.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,
- stateful workplace or enterprise workflows,
- safety and harmful action detection,
- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Benchmark task design.**
- **Evaluation metrics and baseline results.**
- **Failure analysis and limitations.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Benchmark scope may not generalize to all agent settings.
- **Limitation 2:** Evaluation may depend on chosen models and scaffolds.
- **Limitation 3:** Recent results may change as models improve.

General limitation for your thesis:

```text
A benchmark evaluates only a slice of agent behavior.
No single benchmark proves that an agent can perform generalized web automation and data extraction.
```

Therefore, use this paper as a **P2 supporting source**, not as the sole basis for a central claim.

# S5.1 P2 Paper 25 — X-WebAgentBench: A Multilingual Interactive Web Benchmark for Evaluating Global Agentic System

## Metadata

- **Title:** X-WebAgentBench: A Multilingual Interactive Web Benchmark for Evaluating Global Agentic System
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** Findings of ACL 2025
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Corrected action:** Upgrade to peer-reviewed Findings
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** multilingual interactive web-agent benchmark
- **S5.1 role:** web-agent and information-access benchmark
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `xwebagentbenchmultilingualinteractive2025`

---

## Simple understanding

X-WebAgentBench: A Multilingual Interactive Web Benchmark for Evaluating Global Agentic System evaluates web agents or web-based information tasks. Its main value is testing whether agents can search, browse, traverse, retrieve, synthesize, or act on web environments.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark LLM-based agents on web tasks requiring navigation, retrieval, reasoning, and final answer or task completion.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
multilingual interactive web-agent benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Web agents often perform well on short/simple tasks but fail on long-horizon navigation, dynamic pages, multilingual content, broad search, or noisy evidence.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,
- stateful workplace or enterprise workflows,
- safety and harmful action detection,
- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Task/environment design.**
- **Evaluation metrics such as success rate, key-point recall, answer accuracy, NDCG/Recall, or trajectory completion.**
- **Failure analysis for browsing or retrieval mistakes.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Web environments are dynamic and hard to reproduce.
- **Limitation 2:** LLM-based or trajectory-based evaluation can be noisy.
- **Limitation 3:** Strong results may depend on specific scaffolds, browsers, websites, or task selection.

General limitation for your thesis:

```text
A benchmark evaluates only a slice of agent behavior.
No single benchmark proves that an agent can perform generalized web automation and data extraction.
```

Therefore, use this paper as a **P2 supporting source**, not as the sole basis for a central claim.

---

## Venue/status caution

This is safer for stronger thesis claims because the verified status is **Findings of ACL 2025**.

For thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed papers → can support stronger claims
workshop/demo papers → useful but cite as workshop/system evidence
arXiv/technical reports → useful for recent trends, but cite cautiously
unclear/submission papers → verify again before final submission
```

---

## Relation to S5.1

This paper belongs in **S5.1** because S5.1 is about how LLM-based agents are evaluated.

Its role is:

```text
X-WebAgentBench: A Multilingual Interactive Web Benchmark for Evaluating Global Agentic System
→ multilingual interactive web-agent benchmark
→ P2 support for agent benchmark/evaluation discussion
```

Use this paper after explaining its benchmark/evaluation design.  
Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce what it measures first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because:

It directly supports the evaluation of generalized web automation and web data extraction agents.

For your thesis, the paper can support evaluation discussion around:

- what tasks agents should be tested on,
- whether evaluation should be static or interactive,
- whether success should be judged by final answer, trajectory, state, or evidence,
- how to measure failures in planning, grounding, memory, and tool use,
- and why real-world agent evaluation remains unsolved.

---

## How to use this paper in the literature review

Recommended use:

- Use it as **P2 support** in S5.1.
- Extract one exact benchmark property or metric.
- Extract one limitation or failure mode.

---

## Simple understanding

FormFactory: An Interactive Benchmarking Suite for Multimodal Form-Filling Agents contributes to agent evaluation by introducing, analyzing, or standardizing a benchmark or evaluation methodology.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Evaluate LLM-based agents under a specific task family, environment, or scoring protocol.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
multimodal form-filling benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Agent performance depends strongly on environment realism, task horizon, scaffold design, evaluation metric, and failure analysis.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.

## Simple understanding

MM-BrowseComp: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Multimodal Browsing Agents evaluates web agents or web-based information tasks. Its main value is testing whether agents can search, browse, traverse, retrieve, synthesize, or act on web environments.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark LLM-based agents on web tasks requiring navigation, retrieval, reasoning, and final answer or task completion.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
multimodal browsing benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Web agents often perform well on short/simple tasks but fail on long-horizon navigation, dynamic pages, multilingual content, broad search, or noisy evidence.

## Metadata

- **Title:** WebArXiv: Evaluating Multimodal Agents on Time-Invariant arXiv Tasks
- **Year:** 2025/2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2507.00938 / OpenReview 2026 submission
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Unclear
- **Corrected action:** Use cautiously
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** time-invariant arXiv web task benchmark
- **S5.1 role:** web-agent and information-access benchmark
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium
- **BibTeX key:** `webarxivmultimodalagentstime2025`

---

## Simple understanding

WebArXiv: Evaluating Multimodal Agents on Time-Invariant arXiv Tasks evaluates web agents or web-based information tasks. Its main value is testing whether agents can search, browse, traverse, retrieve, synthesize, or act on web environments.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark LLM-based agents on web tasks requiring navigation, retrieval, reasoning, and final answer or task completion.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
time-invariant arXiv web task benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Web agents often perform well on short/simple tasks but fail on long-horizon navigation, dynamic pages, multilingual content, broad search, or noisy evidence.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,
- stateful workplace or enterprise workflows,
- safety and harmful action detection,
- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Task/environment design.**
- **Evaluation metrics such as success rate, key-point recall, answer accuracy, NDCG/Recall, or trajectory completion.**
- **Failure analysis for browsing or retrieval mistakes.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Web environments are dynamic and hard to reproduce.
- **Limitation 2:** LLM-based or trajectory-based evaluation can be noisy.
- **Limitation 3:** Strong results may depend on specific scaffolds, browsers, websites, or task selection.

General limitation for your thesis:

```text
A benchmark evaluates only a slice of agent behavior.
No single benchmark proves that an agent can perform generalized web automation and data extraction.
```

Therefore, use this paper as a **P2 supporting source**, not as the sole basis for a central claim.

## Metadata

- **Title:** MCP-AgentBench: Evaluating Real-World Language Agent Performance with MCP-Mediated Tools
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2509.09734

- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents

- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `mcpagentbenchrealworld2025`

---

## Simple understanding

MCP-AgentBench: Evaluating Real-World Language Agent Performance with MCP-Mediated Tools evaluates tool-using agents, often with structured APIs or MCP servers. Its main value is testing whether agents can select, call, compose, and verify external tools.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark tool-agent interaction under realistic or semi-realistic tool availability, user goals, and execution constraints.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
MCP-mediated real-world agent benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Tool access can improve agent performance and efficiency, but tool retrieval, parameter filling, multi-tool composition, and policy compliance remain difficult.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Tool benchmarks can be easier than real websites when tools are cleanly specified.
- **Limitation 2:** Large tool sets create retrieval difficulty that smaller toy benchmarks may miss.

# S5.1 P2 Paper 44 — SCUBA: Salesforce Computer Use Benchmark

## Metadata

- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2509.26506 / Salesforce technical report
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No

- **Priority:** P2

- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `scubasalesforcecomputeruse2025`

---

## Simple understanding

SCUBA is a Salesforce Computer Use Benchmark for evaluating agents on realistic CRM workflows. Tasks are derived from real user interviews and run in Salesforce sandbox environments.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Evaluate computer-use agents on enterprise CRM tasks involving UI navigation, data manipulation, workflow automation, information retrieval, and troubleshooting.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
Salesforce CRM computer-use benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Open-source computer-use agents that perform well elsewhere can score below 5% in zero-shot SCUBA, while stronger closed-source systems reach up to 39%; demonstrations can improve success up to 50% while reducing time and cost.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,

- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

- **Limitation 1:** It is a Salesforce technical report/preprint in the verified report.

# S5.1 P2 Paper 45 — DRBench: A Realistic Benchmark for Enterprise Deep Research

## Metadata

- **Title:** DRBench: A Realistic Benchmark for Enterprise Deep Research
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** ICLR 2026 Poster
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Corrected action:** Upgrade to peer-reviewed
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** enterprise deep research benchmark
- **S5.1 role:** web-agent and information-access benchmark
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `drbenchrealisticenterprisedeep2026`

---

## Simple understanding

DRBench evaluates enterprise deep research agents. Agents answer high-level strategic questions using both public web data and private enterprise data across files, chat, emails, and cloud storage.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark agents on open-ended enterprise deep research requiring insight recall, factual grounding, distractor avoidance, and report quality.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
enterprise deep research benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Agents are often competent at document retrieval and summarization but miss high-value insights, cite irrelevant evidence, or produce incoherent reports.

## Metadata

- **Title:** TheMCPCompany: Creating General-purpose Agents with Task-specific Tools
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** ICLR 2026 Poster
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Corrected action:** Upgrade to peer-reviewed
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents

- **S5.1 role:** tool-use / MCP / reward evaluation benchmark
- **Priority:** P2

- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `themcpcompanycreatinggeneralpurpose2026`

---

## Simple understanding

TheMCPCompany evaluates agents that use large task-specific MCP tool sets instead of relying mainly on browsers.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Extend TheAgentCompany with MCP servers exposing more than 18,000 tools across Azure, Plane, GitLab, ownCloud, and RocketChat.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
large-scale MCP tool benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Ground-truth tools improve performance and reduce costs; tool retrieval also helps, but agents struggle badly in complex enterprise Azure tasks where tool selection and multi-tool reasoning are hard.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,

- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Tool availability is unusually rich and may not reflect all real deployments.
- **Limitation 2:** The benchmark strongly depends on tool retrieval quality.
- **Limitation 3:** Large tool sets create retrieval and reasoning challenges that remain unsolved.

# S5.1 P2 Paper 52 — Evaluating Long-Context Reasoning in LLM-Based WebAgents

## Metadata

- **Title:** Evaluating Long-Context Reasoning in LLM-Based WebAgents
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** NeurIPS 2025 LAW Workshop
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Workshop
- **Corrected action:** Use as workshop/preprint
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** long-context web-agent evaluation
- **S5.1 role:** web-agent and information-access benchmark
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Medium citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium
- **BibTeX key:** `longcontextreasoningllm2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper evaluates long-context reasoning in LLM-based WebAgents under multi-session, noisy interaction histories.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Create sequentially dependent subtasks where agents must retrieve and apply information from previous trajectories across contexts up to 150k tokens.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
long-context web-agent evaluation
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

WebAgent success drops dramatically as context length increases, often from around 40–50% in baseline settings to below 10% with long noisy contexts; agents get stuck in loops and lose objectives.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,
- stateful workplace or enterprise workflows,
- safety and harmful action detection,
- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Context injection framework with 25k to 150k tokens.**
- **Sequential subtask A1/A2 design requiring memory of earlier interaction.**
- **Error analysis showing looping and objective loss.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Workshop status.
- **Limitation 2:** Live-web evaluation can be noisy and hard to reproduce.
- **Limitation 3:** Implicit RAG gives only modest improvements, so the core limitation remains open.

General limitation for your thesis:

```text

No single benchmark proves that an agent can perform generalized web automation and data extraction.
```

Therefore, use this paper as a **P2 supporting source**, not as the sole basis for a central claim.

This should be cited as a preprint or technical report unless a later peer-reviewed version is confirmed.

For thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed papers → can support stronger claims
workshop/demo papers → useful but cite as workshop/system evidence
arXiv/technical reports → useful for recent trends, but cite cautiously
unclear/submission papers → verify again before final submission
```

---

## Relation to S5.1

This paper belongs in **S5.1** because S5.1 is about how LLM-based agents are evaluated.

Its role is:

```text
CLAWSBENCH: Evaluating Capability and Safety of LLM Productivity Agents in Simulated Workspaces
→ productivity-agent safety and capability benchmark
→ P2 support for agent benchmark/evaluation discussion
```

Use this paper after explaining its benchmark/evaluation design.  
Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce what it measures first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because:

It extends evaluation from web tasks to realistic digital work environments, which matters for generalized automation.

For your thesis, the paper can support evaluation discussion around:

- what tasks agents should be tested on,
- whether evaluation should be static or interactive,
- whether success should be judged by final answer, trajectory, state, or evidence,
- how to measure failures in planning, grounding, memory, and tool use,
- and why real-world agent evaluation remains unsolved.

---

## How to use this paper in the literature review

Recommended use:

- Use it as **P2 support** in S5.1.
- Extract one exact benchmark property or metric.
- Extract one limitation or failure mode.
- Compare it with P0/P1 benchmarks, not as a replacement.
- If the paper is preprint/workshop, phrase claims cautiously.

Suggested thesis sentence:

> CLAWSBENCH: Evaluating Capability and Safety of LLM Productivity Agents in Simulated Workspaces contributes to the evaluation literature by focusing on **productivity-agent safety and capability benchmark**, showing that agent assessment must consider not only final task success but also environment realism, interaction trajectories, tool/action correctness, and reliability.

---

## Comparison with nearby benchmarks

This paper should be compared with benchmarks such as:

```text
WebArena / VisualWebArena
Mind2Web / Online Mind2Web
OSWorld / Windows Agent Arena
AndroidWorld / SPA-Bench
WorkBench / OfficeBench / CLAWSBENCH
Tool-use and MCP benchmarks
Deep research and web search benchmarks
```

The comparison question is:

```text
What does this benchmark evaluate that previous benchmarks do not?
```

---

## Reading decision

- **Keep in S5.1 P2:** Yes
- **Read fully?** Selected sections are enough unless promoted later.
- **Depth needed:** Low to medium
- **Main use:** Support discussion of productivity-agent safety and capability benchmark
- **Most important parts to read:**
  - Abstract and introduction
  - Benchmark/task design
  - Evaluation metrics
  - Main results table
  - Failure analysis
  - Limitations / discussion

---

## One-sentence summary

CLAWSBENCH: Evaluating Capability and Safety of LLM Productivity Agents in Simulated Workspaces is a P2 source for S5.1 because it helps evaluate **productivity-agent safety and capability benchmark**, but it should be cited according to its verified venue/status and used mainly to enrich the benchmark/evaluation discussion.

## Source: `S5_1_P2\paper_first_notes\S5_1_P2_GLOBAL_SYNTHESIS.md`

# S5.1 P2 — Global Synthesis

## What this S5.1 P2 batch covers

The S5.1 P2 papers are mainly about **evaluation and benchmarks for LLM-based agents**. They cover:

1. Web-agent benchmarks.
2. GUI/mobile/desktop benchmarks.
3. Office/workplace/productivity benchmarks.
4. Tool-use and MCP benchmarks.
5. Deep research and broad information-seeking benchmarks.
6. E-commerce and shopping benchmarks.
7. Safety, reward, and agent-as-judge evaluation.
8. Long-context and long-horizon evaluation.
9. Holistic leaderboard and evaluation infrastructure.

## Main thesis value

This batch is important because your thesis cannot only discuss agent architectures. It also needs to explain:

```text
How do we know whether an LLM-based web agent really works?
```

S5.1 should therefore cover:

- task success,
- partial completion,
- action accuracy,
- trajectory quality,
- factuality and citation grounding,
- state-based evaluation,
- safety and harmful action rates,
- cost and latency,
- reproducibility,
- generalization across websites/apps,
- and robustness to long contexts or dynamic environments.

## Recommended writing strategy

Use peer-reviewed benchmarks as the backbone:

```text
TravelPlanner
AndroidWorld
WorkBench
AI Agents That Matter
Spider2-V
CRAB
VisualAgentBench
Windows Agent Arena
SPA-Bench
Agent-as-a-Judge
WebOlympus
SWE-bench Goes Live
X-WebAgentBench
Agent-RewardBench
FormFactory
τ-bench
WebWalker
MCPMark
DRBench
TheMCPCompany
```

Use preprints and workshops for recent trends:

```text
SCUBA
NEWSAGENT
OSWorld-MCP
EcomBench
AGENCYBENCH
Ego2Web
AgentWebBench
CLAWSBENCH
MacroBench
GEM
```

## Key synthesis argument

A strong S5.1 paragraph can say:

```text
Evaluation of LLM-based agents has moved from static answer accuracy toward interactive, stateful, long-horizon, and safety-aware benchmarks. Early web and GUI benchmarks focused on action prediction or task completion, while recent benchmarks increasingly evaluate tool use, MCP invocation, enterprise workflows, deep research, productivity safety, long-context reasoning, and multi-agent coordination. This shift reflects the fact that generalized web automation cannot be measured by final text alone; it requires evaluation of actions, state changes, evidence grounding, cost, safety, and recovery.
```

## Final recommendation

Keep all 56 P2 papers, but do not cite all of them in the final thesis. Use them as a reserve pool for:

- examples,
- taxonomy,
- recent trends,
- evaluation gaps,
- and limitations.


---

## Recommended citation strategy for S5.1

### Use as stronger evidence

Use confirmed peer-reviewed / archival papers for central claims about agent evaluation, benchmark design, and empirical trends. Examples include **TravelPlanner**, **AndroidWorld**, **WorkBench**, **AI Agents That Matter**, **Spider2-V**, **CRAB**, **VisualAgentBench**, **Windows Agent Arena**, **SPA-Bench**, **Agent-as-a-Judge**, **CASA**, **WebOlympus**, **SWE-bench Goes Live**, **X-WebAgentBench**, **Agent-RewardBench**, **FormFactory**, **τ-bench**, **WebWalker**, **MCPMark**, **DRBench**, and **TheMCPCompany**.

### Use as recent-trend / emerging benchmark evidence

Use arXiv/technical-report items for recent benchmark coverage, but avoid making the strongest thesis claims from them alone. This applies especially to very recent 2025–2026 items such as **SCUBA**, **NEWSAGENT**, **OSWorld-MCP**, **EcomBench**, **AGENCYBENCH**, **Ego2Web**, **AgentWebBench**, and **CLAWSBENCH**.

### Use as workshop evidence

Workshop papers are valuable for emerging directions, but mark them clearly as workshop papers. This applies to **GEM**, **MacroBench**, **MCP-Bench**, and **Evaluating Long-Context Reasoning in LLM-Based WebAgents**.

## Final recommendation

S5.1 P2 is strong and very useful for benchmark/evaluation breadth. However, it is mixed in venue quality. For final thesis writing, use peer-reviewed papers as the backbone of S5.1 and use arXiv/workshop papers to cover very recent benchmark trends and open evaluation gaps.

## Recommended citation strategy for S5.1

### Use as stronger evidence

Use confirmed peer-reviewed / archival papers for central claims about agent evaluation, benchmark design, and empirical trends. Examples include **TravelPlanner**, **AndroidWorld**, **WorkBench**, **AI Agents That Matter**, **Spider2-V**, **CRAB**, **VisualAgentBench**, **Windows Agent Arena**, **SPA-Bench**, **Agent-as-a-Judge**, **CASA**, **WebOlympus**, **SWE-bench Goes Live**, **X-WebAgentBench**, **Agent-RewardBench**, **FormFactory**, **τ-bench**, **WebWalker**, **MCPMark**, **DRBench**, and **TheMCPCompany**.

### Use as recent-trend / emerging benchmark evidence

Use arXiv/technical-report items for recent benchmark coverage, but avoid making the strongest thesis claims from them alone. This applies especially to very recent 2025–2026 items such as **SCUBA**, **NEWSAGENT**, **OSWorld-MCP**, **EcomBench**, **AGENCYBENCH**, **Ego2Web**, **AgentWebBench**, and **CLAWSBENCH**.

### Use as workshop evidence

Workshop papers are valuable for emerging directions, but mark them clearly as workshop papers. This applies to **GEM**, **MacroBench**, **MCP-Bench**, and **Evaluating Long-Context Reasoning in LLM-Based WebAgents**.

## Final recommendation

S5.1 P2 is strong and very useful for benchmark/evaluation breadth. However, it is mixed in venue quality. For final thesis writing, use peer-reviewed papers as the backbone of S5.1 and use arXiv/workshop papers to cover very recent benchmark trends and open evaluation gaps.

# S5.1 P2 Paper 01 — Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction

## Metadata

- **Title:** Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction
- **Year:** 2023
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2305.08144
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No
- **Corrected action:** Keep as preprint
- **Thesis section:** S5.1 — Evaluation and Benchmarks for LLM-based Agents
- **Main category:** mobile GUI benchmark
- **S5.1 role:** GUI/mobile-agent benchmark
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `mobileenvbuildingqualified2023`

---

## Simple understanding

Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction evaluates or surveys mobile/Android/smartphone agents. Its main value is testing whether agents can perceive phone screens, understand user goals, and execute multi-step UI actions.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agent evaluation is difficult because agents act over time, use tools, interact with environments, and can fail in many hidden ways.
Paper → This work proposes or analyzes a benchmark, evaluation framework, or evaluation methodology.
Goal → Measure agent capability more realistically than simple text-only QA or isolated reasoning tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the benchmark or evaluation paper measures**.  
Only after that should it be linked to S5.1 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark mobile GUI agents under realistic task, screen, and action conditions.

The paper mainly evaluates this capability:

```text
mobile GUI benchmark
```

For agent evaluation, this matters because a web/GUI/computer-use agent is not judged only by its final answer. It must also be judged by:

```text
task success
+ trajectory quality
+ tool/action correctness
+ factual grounding
+ state changes
+ efficiency/cost
+ safety/reliability
+ recovery from failure
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Mobile agents require strong visual grounding, action selection, state tracking, and recovery; current systems often remain brittle on realistic mobile workflows.

For S5.1, the important point is to identify **what new evaluation gap** this paper covers.  
Most P2 papers in S5.1 extend evaluation into one of these dimensions:

- more realistic environments,
- longer task horizons,
- multimodal perception,
- tool/API/MCP use,
- stateful workplace or enterprise workflows,
- safety and harmful action detection,
- deep research and source-grounded synthesis,
- or cost/reproducibility/leaderboard infrastructure.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Task categories and app/domain coverage.**
- **Observation and action space definition.**
- **Task success or step-level evaluation metrics.**

Also extract, if available:

- number of tasks / instances / environments,
- task domains,
- evaluated models or scaffolds,
- main metrics,
- strongest and weakest model results,
- failure taxonomy,
- and authors' stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Mobile UI tasks may not generalize directly to desktop web automation.
- **Limitation 2:** Benchmarks can become stale as apps update their interfaces.
- **Limitation 3:** Success depends heavily on observation format, action space, and evaluation rules.

General limitation for your thesis:

```text
A benchmark evaluates only a slice of agent behavior.
No single benchmark proves that an agent can perform generalized web automation and data extraction.
```

Therefore, use this paper as a **P2 supporting source**, not as the sole basis for a central claim.

---

## Venue/status caution

This should be cited as a preprint or technical report unless a later peer-reviewed version is confirmed.

For thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed papers → can support stronger claims
workshop/demo papers → useful but cite as workshop/system evidence
arXiv/technical reports → useful for recent trends, but cite cautiously
unclear/submission papers → verify again before final submission
```

---

## Relation to S5.1

This paper belongs in **S5.1** because S5.1 is about how LLM-based agents are evaluated.

Its role is:

```text
Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction
→ mobile GUI benchmark
→ P2 support for agent benchmark/evaluation discussion
```

Use this paper after explaining its benchmark/evaluation design.  
Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce what it measures first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because:

It helps compare web agents with broader GUI/mobile agents, especially for perception, grounding, and action evaluation.

For your thesis, the paper can support evaluation discussion around:

- what tasks agents should be tested on,
- whether evaluation should be static or interactive,
- whether success should be judged by final answer, trajectory, state, or evidence,
- how to measure failures in planning, grounding, memory, and tool use,
- and why real-world agent evaluation remains unsolved.

---

## How to use this paper in the literature review

Recommended use:

- Use it as **P2 support** in S5.1.
- Extract one exact benchmark property or metric.
- Extract one limitation or failure mode.
- Compare it with P0/P1 benchmarks, not as a replacement.
- If the paper is preprint/workshop, phrase claims cautiously.

Suggested thesis sentence:

> Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction contributes to the evaluation literature by focusing on **mobile GUI benchmark**, showing that agent assessment must consider not only final task success but also environment realism, interaction trajectories, tool/action correctness, and reliability.

---

## Comparison with nearby benchmarks

This paper should be compared with benchmarks such as:

```text
WebArena / VisualWebArena
Mind2Web / Online Mind2Web
OSWorld / Windows Agent Arena
AndroidWorld / SPA-Bench
WorkBench / OfficeBench / CLAWSBENCH
Tool-use and MCP benchmarks
Deep research and web search benchmarks
```

The comparison question is:

```text
What does this benchmark evaluate that previous benchmarks do not?
```

---

## Reading decision

- **Keep in S5.1 P2:** Yes
- **Read fully?** Selected sections are enough unless promoted later.
- **Depth needed:** Low to medium
- **Main use:** Support discussion of mobile GUI benchmark
- **Most important parts to read:**
  - Abstract and introduction
  - Benchmark/task design
  - Evaluation metrics
  - Main results table
  - Failure analysis
  - Limitations / discussion

---

## One-sentence summary

Mobile-Env: Building Qualified Evaluation Benchmarks for LLM-GUI Interaction is a P2 source for S5.1 because it helps evaluate **mobile GUI benchmark**, but it should be cited according to its verified venue/status and used mainly to enrich the benchmark/evaluation discussion.
